5/1/24, 9:56 AM - Kusuma Darshi Mca Aurora: Proposed Syllabus 
Module 1: Introduction to Data Science  
Basic Terminology, Data science Venn diagram, Types of Data, The Four Levels of Data. 
Module 2: Importance of Data Science 
Need for Data Science, Five steps of Data Science, Business Intelligence and Process, 
Prerequisites for Data Scientist, Components of Data Science, Tools and Skills needed 
Module 3: Data Science Process 
Introduction, Prior knowledge, Data preparation, Modelling, Application 
Module 4: Data Exploration 
Objectives of Data Exploration, Datasets, Descriptive statistics, data visualization 
Module 5: Methodology for Data Science 
Analytics for Data Science- Data Analytics Examples- Data Analytics Life Cycle 
Module 6: Data Science Tool 
Reading and getting Data into R, ordered and unordered factors, Arrays and matrices, list and data frames, manipulating objects 
Module 7: Data Visualization 
Introduction to data visualization and tableau, Dimensions and measures, basic chart types, dashboards design and principles 
Module 8: Machine learning 
Introduction, modelling process, types of machine learning, semi supervised machine learning. 
linear regression , logistics regression,  
Module 9: Classification Algorithms 
Introduction to classification and algorithms: Decision trees, Naive Bayes, Support vector machines, KNN. 
Module 10: Handling large data on a computer 
Problems faced while handling large data, general techniques while handling large volumes of data, general programming tips for dealing with data, predicting malicious URLS, recommender systems

5/19/24, 5:07 PM - R. Mahesh Mca Aurora: jug1 = int(input("Enter the capacity of jug 1: "))
jug2 = int(input("Enter the capacity of jug 2: "))
goal = int(input("Enter the goal state capacity: "))

def watjug(ch, x, y):
    if ch == 1:  # Fill Jug 1
        if x < jug1:
            return jug1, y
        else:
            print("Sorry rule cannot be applied")
            return x, y
    elif ch == 2:  # Fill Jug 2
        if y < jug2:
            return x, jug2
        else:
            print("Sorry rule cannot be applied")
            return x, y
    elif ch == 3:  # Empty Jug 1
        if x > 0:
            return 0, y
        else:
            print("Sorry rule cannot be applied")
            return x, y
    elif ch == 4:  # Empty Jug 2
        if y > 0:
            return x, 0
        else:
            print("Sorry rule cannot be applied")
            return x, y
    elif ch == 5:  # Pour from Jug 1 to Jug 2 without overflowing Jug 2
        if x > 0 and (x + y) <= jug2:
            return 0, x + y
        elif x > 0 and (x + y) > jug2:
            return x - (jug2 - y), jug2
        else:
            print("Sorry rule cannot be applied")
            return x, y
    elif ch == 6:  # Pour from Jug 2 to Jug 1 without overflowing Jug 1
        if y > 0 and (x + y) <= jug1:
            return x + y, 0
        elif y > 0 and (x + y) > jug1:
            return jug1, y - (jug1 - x)
        else:
            print("Sorry rule cannot be applied")
            return x, y
    elif ch == 7:  # Pour from Jug 1 to Jug 2 and fill Jug 2
        if x > 0 and y < jug2:
            transfer = min(x, jug2 - y)
            return x - transfer, y + transfer
        else:
            print("Sorry rule cannot be applied")
            return x, y
    elif ch == 8:  # Pour from Jug 2 to Jug 1 and fill Jug 1
        if y > 0 and x < jug1:
            transfer = min(y, jug1 - x)
            return x + transfer, y - transfer
        else:
            print("Sorry rule cannot be applied")
            return x, y
    elif ch == 9:  # Transfer all from Jug 2 to Jug 1 if it fits
        if x + y <= jug1 and y > 0:
            return x + y, 0
        else:
            print("Sorry rule cannot be applied")
            return x, y
    else:
        print("INVALID CHOICE")
        return x, y

x = y = 0
while True:
    if x == goal or y == goal:
        print("Goal Reached")
        break
    else:
        ch = int(input("Enter the rule to apply: "))
        x, y = watjug(ch, x, y)
        print(f"Jug 1: {x}, Jug 2: {y}")

data(cars) # make sure data are ready
## Draw scatter plot (Figure 3-23)
C
plot(dist ~ speed, data = cars, main = "Scatter plot", cex=2, las = 1)
## Make a linear model of relationship
cars.lm = lm(dist~ speed, data = cars)
## Use fitted model values to add a line (x = speed, y fitted)
lines(cars$speed, fitted (cars.lm), lwd = 2)

10/6/24, 5:11 PM - R. Mahesh Mca Aurora: # Step 1: Import necessary libraries
import pandas as pd
from sklearn.preprocessing import LabelEncoder
from sklearn.neighbors import NearestNeighbors

# Step 2: Load the data (in this case, 'shopping_trends.csv' file is loaded)
data = pd.read_csv('shopping_trends.csv')

# Step 3: Select important features (columns) for our recommendation system
# We'll use 'Age', 'Purchase Amount', 'Review Rating', and 'Previous Purchases'
features = data[['Age', 'Purchase Amount (USD)', 'Review Rating', 'Previous Purchases']]

# Step 4: Convert 'Gender' to numbers (Male = 0, Female = 1) because algorithms understand numbers better
label_enc = LabelEncoder()
data['Gender_Encoded'] = label_enc.fit_transform(data['Gender'])

# Add this 'Gender_Encoded' column to the features we will use
features['Gender_Encoded'] = data['Gender_Encoded']

# Step 5: Train the KNN model using the selected features
# We set 'n_neighbors=5' to find the 5 most similar customers
knn = NearestNeighbors(n_neighbors=5, metric='euclidean')
knn.fit(features)

# Step 6: Function to recommend items based on a customer's ID
def recommend_items(customer_id):
    # Find the index of the customer in the dataset
    customer_index = data.index[data['Customer ID'] == customer_id].tolist()[0]
    
    # Get the information of this customer (age, purchase history, etc.)
    customer_features = features.iloc[customer_index].values.reshape(1, -1)
    
    # Find the 5 most similar customers
    distances, indices = knn.kneighbors(customer_features)
    
    # Get the items purchased by these similar customers
    neighbor_indices = indices.flatten()
    recommended_items = data.iloc[neighbor_indices]['Item Purchased'].values
    
    return recommended_items

# Step 7: Test the recommendation for Customer ID 1
recommended_items_for_customer_1 = recommend_items(1)
print(recommended_items_for_customer_1)

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
 
data = pd.read_csv("C:/Users/Mahesh Ramineni/Downloads/tips.csv")
colors = {'Thur': 'blue', 'Fri': 'green', 'Sat': 'orange', 'Sun': 'purple'}
plt.scatter(data['day'], data['tip'], c=data['day'].map(colors))
plt.title("Example for Scatter Plot")
plt.xlabel("day")
plt.ylabel("tip")
plt.show()

11/25/24, 8:15 AM - R. Mahesh Mca Aurora: Module 1 :
Introduction to Decision Support
Changing Business Environments and Computerized Decision Support, Managerial Decision Making, Information Systems Support for Decision Making, DSS concepts

Module 2 : 
Business Intelligence and Analytics
A Framework for Business Intelligence (Bl), Business Analytics Overview, Introduction to Big Data Analytics

Module 3 : 
Foundations and Technologies for Decision Making and DSS
Introduction, Phases of the Decision Making, Intelligent Phase, Design phase, Choice phase, Implementation phase, How Decisions are supported, Decision Support Systems- Process, capabilities, classification, components of DSS

Module 4 :
  Data Warehousing
  Definitions and concepts, Data warehousing process overview, Architectures, ETL process,   
  Dataware house development, Implementation issues, The future of Data warehousing

Module 5 :
Business Reporting and Visual Analytics
Business Reporting Definitions and Concepts, A Brief History of Data Visualization, Different Types of Charts and Graphs, Visual Analytics

Module 6 :
Business Performance Management
Closed-Loop BPM Cycle, Performance Measurement, Balanced Scorecards, Six Sigma as a Performance Measurement System

Module 7 :
  Data Mining  
  Data Mining Concepts and Applications,Data mining process, Methods, Software tools, Data   
  Mining Privacy Issues, Myths, and Blunders

Module  8: 
Model-Based Decision Making
Decision Support Systems Modeling: modelling issues, Structure of Mathematical Models, Decision Analysis with Decision Tables and Decision Trees, Multi-Criteria Decision Making With Pairwise Comparisons 

Module 9 :
Knowledge Management 
Knowledge Management Concepts and Definitions, Approaches, Information Technology (IT) in Knowledge Management

Module 10: 
Collaborative systems
Making Decisions in Groups: Characteristics, Process, Benefits, and Dysfunctions, Supporting Groupwork with Computerized Systems, Tools for Indirect Support of Decision Making, Direct Computerized Support for Decision Making: From Group Decision Support Systems to Group Support Systems

1/29/25, 3:23 PM - Kusuma Darshi Mca Aurora: What is Web Mining?

Web Mining is the process of discovering and analyzing useful information from web data.
What are the three types of Web Mining?

Web Structure Mining, Web Content Mining, and Web Usage Mining.
What is the purpose of Web Structure Mining?

It analyzes hyperlinks between web pages to understand website connections.
How does Web Usage Mining help businesses?

It helps in recommendation systems, user experience optimization, and fraud detection.
What are some challenges in Web Mining?

Data complexity, privacy concerns, scalability, and data preprocessing.
What are some real-world applications of Web Mining?

Search engines, e-commerce recommendations, social media analysis, cybersecurity, and healthcare insights.
How does AI enhance Web Mining?

AI helps in sentiment analysis, chatbot implementation, and real-time data processing for better decision-making.

1/29/25, 3:38 PM - Sai Mca E Section: <Media omitted>
1/29/25, 3:42 PM - Vinod kumar: What is sequential pattern mining?
Sequential pattern mining is a data mining technique used to find meaningful patterns in sequential data.

Why is sequential pattern mining important?
It helps in understanding trends, customer behavior, and product patterns to improve decision-making.

What is a sequence in sequential pattern mining?
A sequence is an ordered list of items or events.

What is a pattern in sequential pattern mining?
A pattern is a recurring sequence that appears frequently in the data.

What is support in sequential pattern mining?
Support refers to the frequency of occurrence of a pattern in the dataset.

What is confidence in sequential pattern mining?
Confidence is the probability that a specific event occurs given that another event has already occurred.

What does the GSP algorithm stand for?
GSP stands for Generalized Sequential Patterns.

What is the first step in the GSP algorithm?
Data preparation – transforming data into a suitable format for analysis.

What does candidate generation mean in GSP?
It refers to the process of creating new candidate patterns by combining existing ones.

Why is pruning important in the GSP algorithm?
Pruning removes infrequent patterns to improve efficiency.

How does GSP handle large datasets?
It is designed for efficiency, flexibility, and scalability.

Name one technique used to optimize GSP.
Data compression helps reduce memory usage and speed up processing.

Give one real-world application of sequential pattern mining.
In e-commerce, it helps recommend products based on customer purchase patterns.

What is a challenge in sequential pattern mining?
Handling large datasets efficiently is a key challenge.

What is the final output of the GSP algorithm?
The discovered sequential patterns along with their support and confidence.

2/22/25, 11:44 AM - Naveen CR: # SVM_Grand Hotel Management System

total_amount = 0

def Breakfast():
    global total_amount
    menu = {1: ("Poha", 30), 2: ("Idli-Sambar", 40), 3: ("Aloo Paratha", 50), 4: ("Upma", 35), 5: ("Dosa", 45)}
    while True:
        print("\nBreakfast Menu")
        for key, value in menu.items():
            print(f"{key}. {value[0]} - Rs. {value[1]}")
        print("6. Exit")
        order = int(input("Choose your order: "))
        if order in menu:
            item_name, item_price = menu[order]
            total_amount += item_price
            print(f"Your {item_name} is ready. Price: Rs. {item_price}")
            print(f"Total amount so far: Rs. {total_amount}")  
        elif order == 6:
            break
        else:
            print("Invalid option. Please try again.")
        print("Welcome to SVM Grand Hotel")

def Lunch():
    global total_amount
    menu = {1: ("Dal Tadka", 100), 2: ("Paneer Butter Masala", 150), 3: ("Veg Biryani", 180), 4: ("Aloo Gobi", 120), 5: ("Rajma Chawal", 130)}
    while True:
        print("\nLunch Menu")
        for key, value in menu.items():
            print(f"{key}. {value[0]} - Rs. {value[1]}")
        print("6. Exit")
        order = int(input("Choose your order: "))
        if order in menu:
            item_name, item_price = menu[order]
            total_amount += item_price
            print(f"Your {item_name} is ready. Price: Rs. {item_price}")
            print(f"Total amount so far: Rs. {total_amount}") 
        elif order == 6:
            break
        else:
            print("Invalid option. Please try again.")
        print("Welcome to SVM Grand Hotel")

def Dinner():
    global total_amount
    menu = {1: ("Butter Naan", 50), 2: ("Palak Paneer", 140), 3: ("Chole Bhature", 160), 4: ("Veg Pulao", 170), 5: ("Baingan Bharta", 110)}
    while True:
        print("\nDinner Menu")
        for key, value in menu.items():
            print(f"{key}. {value[0]} - Rs. {value[1]}")
        print("6. Exit")
        order = int(input("Choose your order: "))
        if order in menu:
            item_name, item_price = menu[order]
            total_amount += item_price
            print(f"Your {item_name} is ready. Price: Rs. {item_price}")
            print(f"Total amount so far: Rs. {total_amount}")  
        elif order == 6:
            break
        else:
            print("Invalid option. Please try again.")
        print("Welcome to SVM Grand Hotel")

def main():
    global total_amount
    print("Menu card, please press:\n1 for Breakfast\n2 for Lunch\n3 for Dinner\n4 to Exit")
    while True:
        choose = int(input("Choose your number: "))
        if choose == 1:
            Breakfast()
        elif choose == 2:
            Lunch()
        elif choose == 3:
            Dinner()
        elif choose == 4:
            print(f"Total amount to be paid: Rs. {total_amount}")
            print("Thank you for visiting SVM Grand Hotel!")
            break
        else:
            print("Invalid choice. Please try again.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

2/27/25, 3:04 PM - Sai Mca E Section: Here are the answers along with brief explanations:

### **Aptitude Domain Questions**  

1. **What is the least possible number which when divided by 2,3,4,5,6 leaves remainders 1,2,3,4,5 respectively?**  
   - The required number is of the form **LCM(2,3,4,5,6) - 1**.  
   - **LCM(2,3,4,5,6) = 60**  
   - **60 - 1 = 59**  
   - **Answer: (d) 59**  

2. **450 liters of a mixture of milk and water contain the milk and water in the ratio 9:1. How much water should be added to get a new mixture containing milk and water in the ratio 3:1?**  
   - Milk = **(9/10) × 450 = 405 liters**  
   - Water = **450 - 405 = 45 liters**  
   - Let **x** be the additional water needed.  
   - New ratio equation: **405 / (45 + x) = 3/1**  
   - **405 = 3 × (45 + x)**  
   - **405 = 135 + 3x**  
   - **3x = 270**  
   - **x = 90 liters**  
   - **Answer: (d) 90**  

3. **40% of a number when added to the square of the same number, then it increased to 4040% of itself. The actual number is:**  
   - Let the number be **x**  
   - Given equation: **(40/100) × x + x² = (4040/100) × x**  
   - **0.4x + x² = 40.4x**  
   - **x² = 40.4x - 0.4x**  
   - **x² = 40x**  
   - **x = 40**  
   - **Answer: (c) 40**  

4. **A covers half of his distance with 20 km/h and the rest with 30 km/h. What is the average speed during the whole journey?**  
   - Let the total distance be **2D**  
   - Time taken for first half: **D / 20**  
   - Time taken for second half: **D / 30**  
   - Total time = **D/20 + D/30 = (3D + 2D)/60 = 5D/60 = D/12**  
   - Average speed = **Total Distance / Total Time = 2D / (D/12) = 24 km/h**  
   - **Answer: (b) 24 km/h**  

5. **A can do a piece of work in 10 days, B can do it in 12 days, and C can do it in 15 days. In how many days can they complete the work together?**  
   - Work done per day: **(1/10) + (1/12) + (1/15)**  
   - LCM of **10, 12, 15 = 60**  
   - Work done per day = **(6/60) + (5/60) + (4/60) = 15/60 = 1/4**  
   - Total time = **4 days**  
   - **Answer: (b) 4**  

---

### **Reasoning Domain Questions**  

1. **What is the angle between the hands of the clock at 3:40?**  
   - Angle formula: **θ = |(30 × H - (11/2) × M)|**  
   - **θ = |(30 × 3 - (11/2) × 40)|**  
   - **θ = |90 - 220| = | -130| = 130°**  
   - **Answer: (c) 130°**  

2. **Who is in between C and E?**  
   - Given arrangement:  
     **B C D E F G A**  
   - **Between C and E: D**  
   - **Answer: (c) D**  

3. **Who are the neighbors of G?**  
   - **From the arrangement: B C D E F G A**  
   - **Neighbors of G: E and D**  
   - **Answer: (d) E and D**

**4.** If WRONG is coded as GNORW (reverse order of letters), then **RIGHT** will be coded as **GHIRT**.

- W (23), A (1), L (12), L (12) → **4+7+0+0 = 4700**  

**Answer:** **4700**

import requests

html_doc = """
<html>
<head><title>local Search Example</title></head>
<body>
    <div class="container">
        <h1>Welcome to Local Search</h1>
        <p class="desc">MCA DATASCIENCE YEAR B SECTION.</p>
        <ul id="places">
            <li class="course1">Web Mining</li>
            <li class="course2">Machine Learning</li>
            <li class="course3">Advanced SQL</li>
        </ul>
        <a href="https://aurora.edu.in">Visit Us</a>
    </div>
</body>
</html>
"""

soup = BeautifulSoup(html_doc, 'html.parser')

print(soup.title.text)  # Prints the title text
print(soup.h1.text)  # Prints the first <h1> text
print(soup.p.text)  # Prints the paragraph text
print(soup.find("h1").text)  # Finds and prints first <h1> text
print(soup.find_all("li"))  # Finds and prints all <li> elements
print(soup.a["href"])  # Prints the href attribute of the first <a> tag
print(soup.select("ul#places li.place"))  # Selects list items inside #places
print(soup.body.parent.name)  # Prints the parent of the <body> tag
print(soup.ul.contents)  # Prints the contents of <ul>
print(soup.p.next_sibling)  # Prints the next sibling of <p>
print(soup.ul.li.previous_sibling)  # Fix: Access first <li> inside <ul> before calling previous_sibling

# Fetching links from a live webpage
url = "https://aurora.edu.in"
response = requests.get(url)
soup = BeautifulSoup(response.text, "html.parser")

for link in soup.find_all("a"):
    print(link.get("href"))

3/15/25, 4:01 PM - +91 89772 58957: !pip install thefuzz

from thefuzz import fuzz, process  # Corrected import

schema1 = ["student_id", "student_name", "marks_obtained"]
schema2 = ["stu_id", "name", "total_marks"]

matches = {}  # Changed from tuple to dictionary

for col in schema1:
    best_match = process.extractOne(col, schema2, scorer=fuzz.ratio)
    matches[col] = best_match[0] if best_match[1] > 70 else "no match"  # Fixed condition

print("Schema Matching Results:")
for key, value in matches.items():
    print(f"{key} --> {value}")  # Corrected formatting

3/16/25, 12:48 PM - Naveen CR: # Develop a focused crawler for local search.
!pip install requests beautifulsoup4 fake_useragent selenium webdriver-manager

import requests
from bs4 import BeautifulSoup
from urllib.parse import urljoin
from fake_useragent import UserAgent

BASE_URL = "https://aurora.edu.in"
KEYWORDS = ["admission", "courses", "faculty", "research", "departments"]
MAX_PAGES = 10
HEADERS = {"User-Agent": UserAgent().random}  # Mimic a real browser

def fetch_page(url):
    try:
        response = requests.get(url, headers=HEADERS, timeout=10)
        if response.status_code == 200:
            return response.text
        else:
            print(f"Failed to fetch {url} | Status Code: {response.status_code}")
    except requests.RequestException as e:
        print(f"Error fetching {url}: {e}")

    return None

def extract_links(html, base_url):
    soup = BeautifulSoup(html, "html.parser")
    links = set()
    for a_tag in soup.find_all("a", href=True):
        full_url = urljoin(base_url, a_tag["href"])
        if full_url.startswith(BASE_URL):
            links.add(full_url)
    return links

def is_relevant(content):
    return any(keyword.lower() in content.lower() for keyword in KEYWORDS)

def focused_crawl(start_url):
    visited = set()
    queue = [start_url]

    while queue and len(visited) < MAX_PAGES:
        url = queue.pop(0)
        if url in visited:
            continue
        print(f"Crawling: {url}")
        html = fetch_page(url)
        if html is None:
            continue

        visited.add(url)
        soup = BeautifulSoup(html, "html.parser")
        text_content = soup.get_text()

        if is_relevant(text_content):
            print(f"Relevant Page Found: {url}")

        new_links = extract_links(html, url) - visited
        if not new_links:
            print(f"No new links found on {url}. The site may use JavaScript.")
        queue.extend(new_links)
# Start crawling
focused_crawl(BASE_URL)
